        Chapter 2

        UIM Progression Guide — Chapter 2

        Nov 6, 2026 · @Marcos

        This is Chapter 2 of the guide, written for an Ultimate Ironman. The format is the same as Chapter 1: long thematic blocks, each saying what you carry, what you drop, where you die on purpose and why.

        Where Chapter 1 left you

        You start with about 1.42 million gp from blackjacking to 85 Thieving, 46 Construction, about 46 Crafting, a house in Hosidius with a costume room, workshop, armour case and a magic wardrobe for the graceful outfit you wear, about 62 Agility, a rune pouch, Bones to Peaches, a seed box and log basket, 250 unspent Tithe Farm points kept for the herb sack (it needs 58 Herblore, so you buy it later in this chapter), farming contracts running, Desert Treasure done (the Ancient spellbook is unlocked but not switched on, so you are on the standard book). The coin lines below start from that 1.42 million. The extra 150,000 gp over the 1.27 million this chapter was first planned on replaces about three quarters of the hour of blackjacking the Nardah block used to need (the other quarter hour moves into the top-up before the Yanille block), and it also covers the shop items the moved quests add (moulds, armour pieces, ropes, dyes and the like come to a few thousand). Chapter 1 also leaves a few loose ends for this chapter: 22 noted mahogany logs (20 for the Varrock diary, two for the lectern's planks), Recipe for Disaster's Pirate Pete and Harry's pet fish (both need a fishbowl, 42 Crafting), the amulet of strength (50 Crafting), and Enakhra's Lament if you reach it below 50 Crafting. Lunar Diplomacy will also need about 235,000 more Crafting XP (46 to 61) and about 100,000 more Magic XP, which the end of 2.1 makes up with gold-bar alchs at the Blast Furnace and glassblowing at Port Phasmatys. Chapter 1 finished Children of the Sun, Twilight's Promise, Scrambled!, The Grand Tree, The Eyes of Glouphrie, Garden of Tranquillity, Creature of Fenkenstrain, The Shadow of the Storm, The Feud, Romeo & Juliet, Prince Ali Rescue, The Lost Tribe, The Giant Dwarf, Forgettable Tale of a Drunken Dwarf, Eadgar's Ruse, Watchtower, Plague City, Biohazard, Temple of Ikov, Desert Treasure, Enter the Abyss and Jungle Potion, repaired the Lady Lumbridge for Dragon Slayer I, and started Freeing Pirate Pete (Another Cook's Quest is done). It left you the ring of visibility from Rasolo; the ghostspeak amulet was not carried, so the Lumbridge block below collects a new one from Father Urhney.

        Moved here from Chapter 1. Chapter 1's inventory audit moved these into this chapter, and each has a place below: Rag and Bone Man I (its two Karamja bones in the Dragon Slayer I block, the rest in the block after it), Below Ice Mountain and Black Knights' Fortress (the same block), the crystal saw (Brimstail gives a new one just before the lectern), the eye amulet that starts Temple of the Eye (the Al Kharid loop), the silver sickle and the druid pouch (cast at the Al Kharid furnace, blessed and collected at the Nature Grotto before In Search of the Myreque), Troll Romance and Family Crest's three brothers (their own blocks after Animal Magnetism's magnet), the holy symbol (cast in the Family Crest block, blessed in Edgeville), Monkey Madness I (started with King Narnode in the Edgeville block), Enlightened Journey (2.2, after the Motherlode Mine, with its second trip at the start of the Recipe for Disaster block) and My Arm's Big Adventure (2.2, just before Making Friends with My Arm). Kharedst's memoirs stayed behind as well, so the Kourend trips below use fairy rings AJR and DJR, and Drezel replaces the wolfbane dagger when Canifis needs it.

        What changes for a UIM

        • No servants for planks. The wiki's UIM Construction guide is explicit: a UIM can't send a servant to the bank or the sawmill. Oak-larder and teak-plank butler sessions are replaced by Mahogany Homes contracts, which also earn the plank sack.
        • Falador becomes your respawn. Recruitment Drive gives the Gaze of Saradomin, so from section 2.1 onwards you respawn in Falador. The Falador Castle deathpile you used three times in Chapter 1 is now next to your respawn point, which makes every later planned death a short walk.
        • The cargo hold is narrow storage. The 18 February 2026 update removed the UIM restrictions on the Sailing cargo hold, but it only accepts certain items: salvage, cargo crates, charting supplies, repair kits, cannonballs, cider, beer glasses, whirlpool surprises and trawled fish, and since 5 August 2026 more raw and cooked fish with their tools and bait. Gear, potions, bars, quest items and noted items don't go in.
        • Bars are alched, not banked. At the Blast Furnace a UIM collects each load from the dispenser and casts High Level Alchemy on the gold bars on the spot. There are no banked gold bars for the Dagannoth Rex alchs; keep only the bars a later step uses.
        • Stockpiles become single trips. Four inventories of karambwans, sixteen relicym's balms and banked spares become one trip's worth, bought next to the step that uses them.
        • Money is watched block by block. Section 2.1 spends about 2.4 million and you arrive with about 1.42 million, so each block shows its coin line and section 2.1 has several planned top-ups (about four and a quarter hours before the Yanille and Blast Furnace block, one hour at Rellekka, half an hour before the dragon weapons and about two hours at the end of 2.1), all of them blackjacking: Menaphite thugs in the rogue outfit, about 200,000 gp an hour at 85+ Thieving (the wiki's Menaphite thug page gives about 219,000 gp an hour at best, with the full rogue outfit from 65 Thieving; the rate drops without the outfit or with misclicks, so time your first hour and rescale every top-up below), whenever the stack drops below the next block's needs.

        Chapter 2 at a glance

        SectionMain goalsWhat changes for a UIM
        2.1 Quests and combatConstruction 62, about forty quests, Sailing to 39, Dagannoth Rex for a berserker ring, SlayerMahogany Homes for Construction; Falador respawn and three planned deathpiles (Recruitment Drive, Entrana, the Varrock essence errand); shop-bought Myreque kit; Blast Furnace gold bars alched at the dispenser; blackjacking top-ups for money
        2.2 Barbarian fishing, green dragons and the Hallowed SepulchreLong skilling and Prayer block, Sepulchre for AgilityCargo hold for Sailing supplies and fish only; cut gems and stackable offcuts instead of noted items; Enlightened Journey from two Falador deathpiles; green dragons from an Edgeville deathpile with bones offered from the inventory, or calcified rocks instead; hides a trip at a time; Sepulchre planks from the plank sack
        2.3 More quests and Song of the ElvesQuest block ending in Song of the Elves and PrifddinasThe costume room upgraded first and used as storage; Wilderness steps with minimal gear and a deathpile outside; elf quest items staged through the costume room, deathpiles and Hespori; Superglass Make to 70 Crafting

        2.1: Quests and combat

        Section 2.1 is the longest questing stretch of the guide: Dragon Slayer I, Underground Pass, Heroes' Quest, One Small Favour, Monkey Madness I, the Myreque quests and about thirty more, then the first Sailing levels, a berserker ring from Dagannoth Rex and Slayer to 40. For a UIM it opens differently. The first steps are Mahogany Homes, which gives the Construction and the plank sack a UIM needs for the rest of the game.

        Planned deaths are written into the blocks that need them. The first is for Recruitment Drive, which needs an empty inventory and nothing worn: the Falador Castle deathpile handles it, and after that quest your respawn is Falador, so every later planned death is a short walk from its pile. Dagannoth Rex is the section's one dangerous place: a death in the kings' lair leaves your pile among the three kings, so that block keeps the kit to what you can afford to leave.

        Spellbooks are one more thing to watch. Chapter 1 leaves you on the standard spellbook (the first Mahogany Homes block below checks it), and 2.1 stays on it. The Arceuus teleports cost soul runes (Respawn Teleport 1, Fenkenstrain's Castle Teleport 1, Salve Graveyard Teleport 2), and a UIM buys them in quantity only at the Magic Guild store in Yanille, which needs The Hand in the Sand (49 Crafting); the Mage Training Arena Rewards Guardian sells a few, and Chapter 1 spent those on its own Arceuus trips. You reach that at the end of 2.1, so the Morytania trips before it use the Falador, Lumbridge and Varrock teleports and fairy rings.

        Teleport jewellery runs out over a chapter this long: when a ring of dueling or games necklace crumbles, make the next one the way Chapter 1 did, an emerald ring with Lvl-2 Enchant for a ring of dueling and a sapphire necklace with Lvl-1 Enchant for a games necklace, at a furnace with the moulds.

        RequirementsThe course needs 52 Agility, and you have about 62.

        Optional: the Wilderness Agility Course instead of the top-ups. Every coin line in 2.1 assumes blackjacking, and the default route never takes your items into the Wilderness. If you accept that risk, the Wilderness Agility Course can replace the four top-ups. Once you deposit 150,000 coins into the agility dispenser, every lap pays loot, and the dispenser hands you a looting bag if you don't own one. The wiki's money-making guide puts it at about 1.35 million gp in the first hour and about 2.06 million gp an hour from a 61-lap streak, at Grand Exchange prices; you sell to a shop instead, so expect less. Every lap also gives about 571 Agility XP. Before you go:

        • Put your coins in the Last Man Standing coffer in the Ferox Enclave and carry only the 150,000 gp fee. A UIM killed in the Wilderness loses almost everything, converted to coins for the killer, and the fee is lost on any death and whenever you leave the course.
        • The course is in level 50 to 56 Wilderness, in a Singles-plus area, and skeletons attack you there. The wiki's UIM walkthrough plays it on a mass world with Protect from Ranged on.
        • The loot goes into the looting bag noted, and a UIM gets it out only by destroying the bag in the Wilderness, outside the course (it can't be destroyed inside, and destroying it in the Ferox Enclave, within two tiles of the ditch or outside the Wilderness deletes the contents). Other players see the loot on the ground at once, so pick it up straight away. Sell the alchables at Martin Thwait's Lost and Found in the Rogues' Den (50 Thieving and 50 Agility), which pays the High Alchemy value for the first of each item and 60% after that.
        • Logging out inside the course costs 10 laps of your streak, and leaving it resets the streak.

        If you take this option, skip the blackjacking top-ups as far as your coin stack allows, and read each block's coin line as the minimum to hold before that block.

        Mahogany Homes to the plank sack

        A UIM can't send a servant to the bank or the sawmill, so all the Construction here comes from Mahogany Homes. You start at 46 Construction from Chapter 1 and finish this block with the plank sack and 62 Construction.

        Gate inChapter 1 finished; 46 Construction; on the standard spellbook; three steel bars in hand.
        Carry inthe core kit plus a hammer, a saw, your axe, the log basket and three steel bars; about 15 free slots for a contract's planks.
        Exit62 Construction and the plank sack in your core kit; the costume room's oak and teak storage tiers; about 760,000 gp; about 14/28.
        Carries forwardthe plank sack (to the end of the guide).

        The wiki's point values set the plan. Novice contracts (oak planks, from level 20) pay 3 carpenter points each; Adept contracts (teak planks, from level 50) pay 4; the plank sack costs 350 points, which is 88 Adept contracts from zero or 117 Novice ones. Each contract needs up to about 12 oak planks or 15 teak planks, plus a steel bar now and then, and the homeowner's cup of tea restores your run energy to full. A contract can't be cancelled, so check your planks and steel bars before you accept one, not after. Nails aren't needed, so don't buy any. How often a contract asks for a steel bar depends on the contract and the town, so three bars last a good while but are worth recounting between contracts.

        Your kit through this block is the core kit plus a hammer, a saw, your axe, the log basket and three steel bars. Chapter 1 spent its noted steel bars on clockworks, so smelt three from iron ore and coal at any furnace before you start, or superheat them (spares can wait in the Blast Furnace bar dispenser). You need about 15 free slots for a contract's planks; the log basket's 28 logs don't count against that, because it stores logs automatically while it is open. This block comes first on purpose. The lectern later in this section needs a base level of 62 that only these contracts supply, and almost every later block that builds, repairs or bridges something (the Sepulchre planks in 2.2 and the plank jobs after it) draws its planks from the sack you buy at the end of it.

        Before the first contract, check that you are on the standard spellbook. Finishing Desert Treasure switches you to the Ancient book, and Chapter 1 switches you back at the pyramid altar straight away, but check anyway, because the next blocks need standard spells (Mahogany Homes alone sends you between Falador, Varrock, East Ardougne and Hosidius, and the house teleport puts you in Hosidius, where Angelo is): teleports and Teleport to House, Lvl-1, Lvl-2 and Lvl-3 Enchant, Fire Bolt for Troll Romance, and later Crumble Undead and Telekinetic Grab. If your book does show Ancient spells, pray at the altar in the Ancient Pyramid to swap back; the same altar switches you to the Ancient spells when a later block wants them.

        Take the first contract and do Novice contracts to 50

        1. Teleport to Falador and talk to Amy, south of Falador Park. Only your first contract has to come from her, because she signs you up; after that, Marlo in Varrock, Ellie in East Ardougne and Angelo in Hosidius hand them out too. Choose Novice, which needs 20 Construction and so is well within your 46. A contract can't be cancelled, so finish each one before taking the next.
        2. Work the Novice contracts from Hosidius, where your house already stands, and take them from Angelo. A contract can send you to any of the four towns (Falador, Varrock, East Ardougne or Hosidius), so teleport to whichever town it names. A Falador contract sends you to the south-east of the city, a Varrock one to the north-east or the south, an East Ardougne one somewhere across the city, and a Hosidius one to the south or centre of town. Once you have done one, Angelo's Last-tier contract option gives you a new contract at the tier of your previous one, and that is all you need until 50. Some clients have the repair upstairs, such as Norman in Falador and Jess in East Ardougne, so check the upper floor before you leave a house.
        3. Chop oaks at the Woodcutting Guild (it has four oak trees and its own sawmill on the east side), turn them into oak planks at the guild's sawmill operator, and come back to that sawmill for more planks between contracts. Keep about 15 oak planks in your inventory and the log basket full of oak logs, so that after each contract you only need the sawmill, not the trees. The guild asks for 60 Woodcutting at the gate, and a boosted level counts. The operator won't take noted logs, so every log you carry to him must be a plain one; the noted mahogany logs from Chapter 1 are dealt with in the lectern block. The fairy ring route is AKR to the Hosidius Vinery and a run west.

        Stop taking Novice contracts at 50 Construction. By the wiki's average of about 1,894 XP a Novice contract (completion bonus included), 46 to 50 is about 18 contracts, which have also given you 54 carpenter points toward the plank sack. Keep the log basket open while you chop, so each log drops straight into it, and top the oak planks in your inventory back up at the sawmill whenever a contract leaves you short of the 15 you keep.

        Done when50 Construction, with a first stack of carpenter points.

        Coins: about 1,375,000 gp · Slots: about 13/28 plus planks · Requirements: 20 Construction for Novice contracts; 60 Woodcutting for the Woodcutting Guild

        Adept contracts to the plank sack

        From 50, take Adept contracts. Teak planks are the cost: the sawmill charges 500 gp per teak plank. The wiki's Mahogany Homes averages for an Adept contract are about 3,260 XP, completion bonus included, and 287.9 XP per teak plank, so about 11 planks a contract. Teak at 500 gp a plank makes gold, not time, the limit here, which is why the coin line drops across this block. From your 54 Novice points, 350 points is about 74 Adept contracts: about 840 teak planks, roughly 420,000 gp, and about 241,000 XP, which carries you from 50 to 62 by the time you own the sack.

        The wiki's UIM Construction guide points to the Avium Savannah teaks for this: they are far from a sawmill, which is exactly what the log basket is for. The wiki's teak page lists six teak trees east of Locus Oasis, close to fairy ring AJP and the quetzal network. Varlamore is already open: Chapter 1 finished Children of the Sun and Twilight's Promise, so Regulus Cento outside Varrock's east gate will fly you to Civitas illa Fortis and the quetzal network is open.

        1. For the savannah itself, take fairy ring AJP to the middle of the savannah and chop teaks with the basket open, so logs go straight into it.
        2. Make the planks at the Woodcutting Guild's sawmill (fairy ring AKR to the Hosidius Vinery, then run west). The quetzal to the Auburnvale sawmill works now too, but the fairy ring route needs no detour. The Auburnvale route needs Children of the Sun, which you finished in Chapter 1. Once you own the plank sack, planks made at a sawmill go into it automatically, so your inventory stops filling with them.
        3. Contracts can send you to any of the four towns, so teleport to the client's town with whatever you have. With about 15 planks in your inventory and 28 teak logs in the basket, one chopping trip covers two contracts: do the first, take the basket's logs back to the sawmill, plank them, and do the second.

        At 350 points, buy the plank sack from the Mahogany Homes reward shop. It holds 28 planks in one slot, they weigh nothing in it, and contracts take planks from it automatically. It joins your core kit; keep it from here to the end of the guide. Don't spend points on anything else until you own it. The reward shop in Falador sells it (Amy runs it), stock is unlimited, and you can own only one at a time. It keeps its planks through a death, but like the rest of your kit it ends up in your deathpile.

        After the sack, check your level: the mahogany eagle lectern in the house block needs a base of 62 Construction before a cup of tea (+2) and a crystal saw (+3) lift it to 67. By the wiki's averages the 74 contracts above already end at about 342,000 XP, past the 333,804 that 62 needs. If your contracts came in below the average and you are short, keep taking Adept contracts until 62, at about 3,260 XP each. The wiki says invisible boosts such as the crystal saw don't work on contracts, so don't count on one to reach these levels. The coin figures in this chapter keep about 150,000 gp for up to 26 such contracts (about 300 teak planks); if you don't need them, that money is spare.

        Done whenthe plank sack is in your core kit and you are at 62 Construction (base).

        Coins: about 760,000 gp · Slots: about 14/28 with the plank sack · Requirements: 50 Construction for Adept contracts; 35 Woodcutting for teaks; Children of the Sun (done in Chapter 1) for Varlamore

        Build the costume room's cheap storage tiers

        At 62 Construction the lower tiers of the costume room's storage furniture are in reach and cost almost nothing, so build them now instead of waiting for the full upgrade in 2.3. They give the capes, costumes, mage sets and clue rewards you pick up in 2.1 and 2.2 a home until then.

        Build these in the costume room of your Hosidius house with the hammer and saw. Each upgrade is built from the tier before it:

        PieceConstructionPlanksHolds
        Treasure chest (oak)482 oakBeginner and easy clue rewards
        Toy box (oak)502 oakHoliday toys
        Cape rack (oak)544 oakEvery cape except Capes of Accomplishment
        Magic wardrobe (carved oak, then teak)51, then 606 oak, then 4 teak21 mage sets, up from the oak wardrobe's 7
        Fancy dress box (oak, then teak)44, then 622 oak, then 2 teak4 types of costumes

        That is 16 oak planks and 6 teak planks, made at the Woodcutting Guild's sawmill like the contract planks: about 7,000 gp in fees (250 gp an oak plank, 500 gp a teak plank). The 150,000 gp contract reserve above covers it, so the coin lines that follow don't change; if extra contracts used the reserve up, the first blackjacking top-up runs a couple of minutes longer. Move any cape, costume or clue reward you are carrying into its piece as soon as it is built. The teak armour case (64), teak cape rack (63), teak treasure chest (66) and everything above them wait for 2.3.

        Done whenthe five pieces are built.

        Coins: about 760,000 gp (the fees come out of the contract reserve) · Slots: about 14/28 · Requirements: 48, 50, 51, 54, 60 and 62 Construction for the pieces

        Wiki referencesMahogany HomesCostume roomUltimate Ironman Guide/ConstructionTeak plankWoodcutting GuildLog basket

        Combat to 50 Attack at the sand crabs

        Chapter 1 trains Attack only through quest rewards, so you start 2.1 at about 46 Attack, and Iban's staff (Underground Pass) and the ancient staff (the sandstone block) both need 50. Train it now, while your house is still in Hosidius. Defence doesn't need training here: Holy Grail (15,300 XP), Nature Spirit and The Fremennik Trials leave you at about 33, which covers the initiate armour's 20, and Dragon Slayer I's 18,650 Defence XP takes you to 40 for the green d'hide body and the rune platelegs. If you are a little short when you buy the body, finish on these crabs on the defensive style.

        Gate inthe plank sack bought and 62 Construction; the rune sword (40 Attack); the house in Hosidius.
        Carry inthe core kit, the rune sword worn, any armour you have and a few pieces of food: about 12/28.
        Exit50 Attack; nothing spent.
        Carries forwardnothing new.

        Sand crabs on the Hosidius coast

        Sand crabs (level 15, 60 Hitpoints, max hit 1, one attack every 2.4 seconds) line the southern coast of Hosidius, a short walk from your house. Until something wakes them they look like harmless sandy rocks and stay off the minimap, then attack as you walk past. The wiki rates them as AFK training: stand among a cluster and they attack you. After about ten minutes they stop being aggressive; walk away until you are about a minimap's width off, come back, and they attack again. An altar near the Hosidius crabs lets you top up Prayer on the walk, though you don't need it here. Avoid Crabclaw Isle (10,000 coins and often full); the Avium Savannah crabs in Varlamore are a quieter alternative. Skip the crabs at Outer Fortis: they start awake and aren't aggressive, so they can't be used for AFK training.

        Wield the rune sword on an accurate style and train Attack from about 46 to 50 (roughly 31,500 XP, plus Hitpoints XP on the side); plan on about an hour, and alch or fletch nothing here, since the crabs give you nothing to manage. Their 1-damage hits mean food is only a safety margin, and a UIM risks nothing: no item drops worth keeping and no deathpile to plan. Hosidius is the busier of the two spots, so a crowded beach is the reason to switch to the Varlamore one.

        Done whenyou are 50 Attack.

        Coins: about 760,000 gp, unchanged · Slots: about 12/28 · Requirements: 40 Attack for the rune sword

        Wiki referencesSand CrabAttackDefence

        Dragon Slayer I, Crandor and the first lamps

        This block finishes Dragon Slayer I, takes two Karamja medium tasks on Crandor, collects an easy diary lamp and kills the two Karamja monsters whose bones Rag and Bone Man I needs. It sits here because the quest's 18,650 Defence XP is what lifts you to 40 for the green d'hide body and the rune platelegs, because the two Karamja medium tasks on Crandor need the quest started, and because the Chronicle charges bought in it are the teleport network the rest of the chapter leans on.

        Gate in32 quest points; 40 Ranged for the green d'hide body, and 40 Defence, which the quest's own Defence XP gives you; the plank sack; the Lady Lumbridge already repaired with Klarense in Chapter 1.
        Carry inthe core kit and the plank sack, plus the Crandor map, your rune sword, the anti-dragon shield, a dozen cooked swordfish, two prayer potions, a ring of recoil and one bread for Below Ice Mountain: about 23/28.
        ExitDragon Slayer I done, the green d'hide body worn, a Vannaka task finished, the Herblore lamp used and 100 Chronicle charges; the giant bat wing and the monkey paw for Rag and Bone Man I; about 740,000 gp; about 20/28.
        Carries forwardthe giant bat wing and the monkey paw (Rag and Bone Man I), the bread (Below Ice Mountain) and the Chronicle charges.

        You arrive with the core kit and the plank sack. Add the Crandor map, your rune sword, the anti-dragon shield, a dozen cooked swordfish from your noted stack (unnote the stack at the bank chest in the Hosidius Kitchen before you add anything else, since it gives as many as fit, and cook a dozen and drop the extra raw ones before you leave, on its range if you have the Kourend & Kebos easy diary, which the wiki makes those ranges need, or on a fire with your tinderbox if that diary is still open), two prayer potions and a ring of recoil, and buy one bread at Logava Gricoller's Cooking Supplies in Hosidius (12 gp): Below Ice Mountain's steak sandwich needs it in the next block, and the wiki lists no bread seller on that block's Misthalin and Falador route. Ned only sails once the ship is repaired, and Chapter 1 already repaired the Lady Lumbridge with Klarense in Port Sarim, so the ship needs nothing from you beyond handing Ned the map. That is about 23 slots.

        If you lose the shield in a death, Oziach's shop in Edgeville sells anti-dragon shields at 26 gp, a cheap replacement. The one risk is Elvarg. A death on Crandor leaves your pile on the island, which you can reach again through the hidden wall door you unlock in this block, but go in with full Hitpoints, the anti-dragon shield worn and the recoil on. Elvarg is level 83 and her melee hits up to 8, but her dragonfire is the real threat: unprotected it reaches 70, and the shield cuts it to 10. Each breath also drains a tenth of your remaining Prayer points, so don't be surprised if the potions are needed sooner than the food, and walking back into her arena triggers an immediate breath. If you have to leave the lair before she is dead, the wiki's quick guide warns that the ship may need repairing again, so avoid leaving. The way back that doesn't need the ship is the dungeon below the Karamja volcano: the wiki's Karamja diary note describes it as through the hidden wall, past the lesser demons and up the rope to Crandor. The wiki's own order opens the wall south of Elvarg's cave before the fight, which is worth doing first if you want that way back in place.

        Collect the lamps and buy Chronicle cards

        1. Teleport to Lumbridge and claim the Lumbridge & Draynor easy diary reward from Mikasi, east of the castle courtyard, and put its lamp on Herblore straight away, so it never takes a slot. The lamp gives 2,500 XP and works only on a skill at level 30 or above, which Herblore is; it is the skill you want because the herb sack later needs 58. The Kandarin easy reward waits for Harry's pet fish at the end of 2.1, so skip Camelot for now.
        2. Run to Draynor, recruit Ned for Dragon Slayer I (his house is in the east of the village), and buy 100 teleport cards from Diango, who runs the toy store in Draynor, at 150 gp each; he stocks exactly 100, so one visit covers it. Buy the Chronicle back from him first (300 gp) if a Chapter 1 planned death destroyed it: its saved charges come back with it. Diango refuses to sell a second Chronicle while you still hold one, so only ask if it was destroyed. Use each card on your Chronicle as you buy it: the charges live in the Chronicle, so 100 cards cost no slots. The book holds up to 1,000 charges, each teleport uses one and drops you just outside the Champions' Guild, and it does not work in the Wilderness, which matters for the Wilderness steps in 2.3.
        Done whenthe lamp is spent on Herblore, Ned is recruited and the Chronicle holds 100 charges.

        Crandor and Elvarg

        1. Board the Lady Lumbridge at Port Sarim, talk to Ned and agree to sail, giving him the map when he asks. Kill a giant bat on the island for Rag and Bone Man I's giant bat wing: Chapter 1 started the quest with the Odd Old Man, and the wiki lists giant bats on Crandor and in the Karamja dungeon below it. Rag and Bone Man II's moss giant waits for the Varrock Sewers, because that quest starts only after Rag and Bone Man I.
        2. Walk down to Elvarg's lair, climb back up the rope to Crandor once for the diary, then return and kill Elvarg behind the anti-dragon shield. The diary task needs only that Dragon Slayer I has been started, and the wiki's quick guide has you enter the hole at the top of the volcano, watch the cutscene and climb out again.
        3. Afterwards, open the hidden wall door from the lair side, which is the second Karamja medium task here and your shortcut if you ever need to come back. The wall is south of Elvarg's cave and the task also requires only that the quest has been started.
        4. On your way out through Karamja, step into the Fight Cave and leave for its diary task (wait until the first wave has begun before you go, because the wiki says the task counts once it has started, and use the Cave, since the Pits refuse you unless another player is present), then kill a monkey for Rag and Bone Man I's monkey paw: the wiki lists monkeys west of the volcano by the path to Brimhaven and next to fairy ring CKR.
        Done whenElvarg is dead, both Karamja medium tasks and the Fight Cave task are done, and you hold the giant bat wing and the monkey paw.

        Finish Dragon Slayer I and take a Slayer task

        1. Use the Soul Wars minigame teleport and step through the portal to Edgeville, as in Chapter 1, and talk to Oziach, who lives just north-west of the village, to complete Dragon Slayer I. Buy a green d'hide body from his shop (10,140 gp; he holds two) and wear it.
        2. Then get a task from Vannaka, and do it in full; Slayer rewards later in this section start from these points.
        3. Drop the Crandor map leftovers, the head and any spare quest items before you leave Edgeville.
        Done whenDragon Slayer I is complete, the green d'hide body is worn and Vannaka's task is finished.

        Coins: about 740,000 gp · Slots: about 23/28 for Crandor, 20/28 after · Requirements: 32 quest points for Dragon Slayer I; the wiki recommends 33 Magic (or 10,000 gp instead) and 37 Prayer

        Wiki referencesDragon Slayer IKaramja DiaryRag and Bone Man IChronicle

        Rag and Bone Man I, Below Ice Mountain and Black Knights' Fortress

        Dragon Slayer I ends in Edgeville, which is where all three of these continue. None needs more than a few slots at once in this order: Below Ice Mountain's recruits first, Black Knights' Fortress while you are in Falador for Burntof, then Rag and Bone Man I's bones from Draynor to Varrock, each dropped into a pot of vinegar the moment it falls. Chapter 1 started Rag and Bone Man I and Below Ice Mountain and left the rest here.

        Gate inRag and Bone Man I and Below Ice Mountain started in Chapter 1; the giant bat wing, the monkey paw and the bread from the Dragon Slayer I block; 12 quest points for Black Knights' Fortress; 10 Mining (boostable) for Camdozaal's pillars.
        Carry inthe core kit and the plank sack, a pickaxe, a knife, a tinderbox, an axe, the two Karamja bones and the bread: about 20/28. Eat or drop what is left of the Elvarg food first.
        ExitBelow Ice Mountain, Black Knights' Fortress and Rag and Bone Man I done; Rag and Bone Man II started; two ropes for Underground Pass; about 745,000 gp; about 18/28.
        Carries forwardthe two ropes (Underground Pass).

        Edgeville and Barbarian Village: Below Ice Mountain's recruits

        1. Talk to Marley by the yew trees in Edgeville, in the ruins south of the bank. The wiki says he has to come first, or the cook won't give you the sandwich recipe. He was banned from the Blue Moon Inn and the cook won't make him another sandwich, so you bring one yourself.
        2. Walk south to Barbarian Village. Talk to Checkal near the pottery oven building, then to Atlas in the Long Hall, and finish his training; take a cooked meat and a beer from the tables there. Go back to Checkal and use the Flex emote when he asks; the training cutscene is what unlocks it.
        3. Buy a bronze med helm at Peksa's Helmet Shop in the village (24 gp) for Black Knights' Fortress.
        4. Teleport to Varrock and ask the cook in the east room of the Blue Moon Inn whether he can make you a steak sandwich. Use the knife on the cooked meat with the bread in your inventory, walk back to Marley in Edgeville and give him the sandwich. Burntof, Checkal and Marley can be visited in any order, but Marley must come before the cook.
        Done whenCheckal, Atlas and Marley have agreed to help Willow, and the beer is still in your inventory.

        Falador: Burntof, Black Knights' Fortress and Camdozaal

        1. Teleport to Falador. Upstairs in the western tower of the White Knights' Castle (the second floor, in UK numbering), talk to Sir Amik Varze to start Black Knights' Fortress. Buy an iron chainbody at Wayne's Chains in the south of the city (210 gp) and pick a cabbage at the farm south of Falador; the wiki says a cabbage from Draynor Manor doesn't work, and another from the same farm replaces one you lose.
        2. In the Rising Sun Inn, give Burntof the beer from the Long Hall (Emily or Kaylee sell one for 3 gp if you drank it) and play his rock-paper-scissors game; any choice works.
        3. Wear the bronze med helm and the iron chainbody, walk to the fortress north of Falador and follow the wiki's route: the sturdy door on the south side, the push-wall, the ladders down to the grill on the eastern wall, where you listen; then back out by the same route, through the eastern door into the dining area and past the guard (choose to go in anyway), north up the ladder, then east and south to push the wall, and on to the hole, where you use the cabbage. Don't eat it: right-click the cabbage and use it on the hole rather than left-clicking it. The Black Knights are level 33 and you can run past them. Only the helm and body slots matter for the disguise; anything else you wear is ignored. The wiki describes the first push as the wall straight ahead just inside the entrance and the second as the wall on the upper landing, which opens onto the hole.
        4. Go to the Ruins of Camdozaal entrance on the west side of Ice Mountain and talk to Willow. After the cutscene, mine the four structural pillars in the corners with your pickaxe instead of fighting the level 25 Ancient Guardian. The guardian hits you the whole time you mine, so eat what is left of the Elvarg food while you mine, and afterwards loot Willow's bag. If your Mining is below 10 and you can't boost it, the wiki notes a safespot against the east wall near the exit. That completes Below Ice Mountain: 1 quest point, 2,000 coins, 1,200 Mining XP and access to the Ruins of Camdozaal.
        5. Teleport to Falador and report to Sir Amik to complete Black Knights' Fortress: 3 quest points, 2,500 coins and two lamps of 1,000 XP each in a combat skill; put both on Prayer (the lamps give 1,000 XP in one combat skill, and Prayer is among them). Drop the helm and the chainbody.
        Done whenBelow Ice Mountain and Black Knights' Fortress are complete.

        Rag and Bone Man I

        The wiki's list is eight bones, eight pots, eight jugs of vinegar, eight logs and a tinderbox. Make the pots of vinegar first, so that each bone goes into one as it drops and the eight never take more than eight slots. Every bone is a guaranteed drop from its monster, and the wiki warns that emptying a bone from its vinegar loses it, so don't click Empty on a pot you have filled:

        1. Teleport to Lumbridge and buy eight pots at the general store north of the castle (1 gp each).
        2. Walk west to Draynor. Fortunato's Wine Shop, in the marketplace just west of the bank, sells jugs of vinegar for 1 gp, and only while Rag and Bone Man I or II is running (talk to him and mention the Odd Old Man first): buy them one at a time and pour each into a pot as you buy it. Use the giant bat wing and the monkey paw on two of the pots. Buy two ropes from Ned for Underground Pass (18 gp each at his shop, in the east of the village).
        3. Kill a level 2 goblin south of the village for the goblin skull. In the Lumbridge Swamp, kill a unicorn, a giant rat and a big frog (the wiki says only the level 10 and 24 big frogs drop the leg; small and giant frogs don't). Then go to the south-east Varrock mine and the forest south of it for the bear ribs, and to the sheep pen south-west of the mine for the ram skull. Put each bone in a pot of vinegar as soon as you pick it up.
        4. Walk to the Odd Old Man in Silvarea, north-east of Varrock by the Limestone Mine. For each bone, put a log on the pot-boiler next to him, use a bone in vinegar on it, light the log and wait about 12 seconds for the fire to burn out before you take the pot back (hopping worlds after you light it skips the wait). Chop the logs nearby one at a time, so they never need more than one slot.
        5. Talk to him to complete Rag and Bone Man I (1 quest point, 500 Cooking and 500 Prayer XP; you may polish and hand in bones at any time, but doing all eight together is faster), then again to start Rag and Bone Man II. Its bones come from the blocks below, and the wiki confirms he accepts them in small batches, so you never collect a stack.
        Done whenRag and Bone Man I is complete, Rag and Bone Man II is started and the two ropes are in your inventory.

        Coins: about 745,000 gp · Slots: about 20/28 arriving; about 27/28 at the peak, with the eight bones in vinegar and the ropes · Requirements: Rag and Bone Man I and Below Ice Mountain started; 12 quest points for Black Knights' Fortress; 10 Mining (boostable) for the pillars, or a level 25 fight

        Wiki referencesBelow Ice Mountain/Quick guideBlack Knights' Fortress/Quick guideRag and Bone Man I/Quick guideRag and Bone Man IIFortunatoPeksa's Helmet ShopWayne's ChainsNed

        Underground Pass, the gold leaf and your house tablets

        RequirementsIt comes here because the staff needs the 50 Attack from the crabs, because the lectern's tablets become your travel for every herb and tree run afterwards, and because Iban's staff is the weapon the Haunted Mine and Dagannoth Rex later ask for.

        This block covers the house furniture and the next quests. It completes Underground Pass and upgrades Iban's staff, starts Between a Rock..., builds the mahogany eagle lectern and makes about a hundred teleport-to-house tablets. It is the most expensive block of the section: 200,000 gp for the staff upgrade and 130,000 gp for the gold leaf. Check your stack before you start; if you're under about 450,000 gp, do the blackjacking top-up first (see the end of this section).

        Gate inabout 450,000 gp or more (otherwise the blackjacking top-up first); 62 Construction base; 50 Attack from the sand crab block; 25 Ranged and Biohazard; 50 Magic and 50 Attack for the staff; the two ropes from Ned.
        Carry inthe plank sack filled from your next sawmill trip; for the pass, two ropes, a bow with 1 to 5 metal-tipped arrows, a spade, a dozen pieces of food and two prayer potions: about 18/28.
        ExitUnderground Pass done and Iban's staff (u) charged; the house in Rellekka with the menagerie, the kitchen pieces, the study and the lectern; about 100 house tablets; Between a Rock... started up to Rolad; about 370,000 gp; about 14/28.
        Carries forwardthe house tablets, one soft clay for the rod mould, Iban's staff (u), and the ring of visibility from Rasolo.

        Move the house and build the kitchen and menagerie

        1. With the plank sack filled from your next sawmill trip, pay an estate agent to move your house to Rellekka, as you did in 1.3; it puts you next to Keldagrim and Trollheim for the next two blocks.
        2. In the house, build a menagerie with a pet house, so a cat can live there between the quests that need one instead of following you everywhere.
        3. In the kitchen, which Chapter 1's larder training already built, build the pieces the lectern block's tea needs: oak shelves 2 for the kettle, teapot and porcelain cup, a pump and drain for water and a stove to boil the kettle. The wiki's tea page says the stove has to be a built range or oven and not a firepit, and that making the tea takes 20 Cooking. Leave a larder standing for the tea leaves.
        4. Build a small oven as the stove: it needs 4 steel bars and 24 Construction, and any stove boils the kettle for tea, so a bigger range only costs bars. Smelt the four bars from iron ore and coal before you build: Ordan at the Blast Furnace in Keldagrim, next to your new house, sells both, and the furnace there needs only one coal per bar. Below 60 Smithing it charges 2,500 gp per 10 minutes, and because you have no ice gloves yet, cool the bars with a bucket of water before you take them.

        Skip the bedrooms and the dining room's bell-pull: they are for the servant, and a UIM's servant can't fetch planks.

        Done whenthe house stands in Rellekka with a menagerie and pet house, oak shelves 2, a pump and drain, a small oven and a larder.

        Complete Underground Pass

        RequirementsUnderground Pass: 25 Ranged.

        You started Underground Pass with King Lathas in 1.4. The wiki's item list is two ropes (you lose one each time you fall from the rope swing), a bow with 1 to 5 non-poisoned metal-tipped arrows (crossbows, broad arrows and ogre arrows don't work; the two ropes are the ones from Ned; Chapter 1 dropped its bow and steel arrows, so buy a shortbow and ten iron arrows at Lowe's Archery Emporium in Varrock first, and keep the bow and the spare arrows: Horror from the Deep uses up an arrow and Regicide needs a bow and arrows), and a spade; a plank is picked up inside, and the quest hands you its other items as you go. Bring a dozen pieces of food and two prayer potions and keep your Hitpoints above 15 when you cross the grid. Koftik stands beside the cave entrance at the far west of West Ardougne, and the wiki warns of a point later in the pass after which there is no turning back, so have everything with you first. Light the arrow with your tinderbox before you shoot the guide rope, and remember that falling into the pit at the rope swing costs you that rope, which is why the guide has you carry two. On the last orb path, don't pick up the orb lying on the ground: disarm the trap underneath it first, then take the orb.

        That is about 18 slots arriving, and the pass fills the rest with quest items you use and drop as you go. A death inside leaves your pile in the pass, so eat early in the fights near the end. If you die on the grid, the wiki says your items stay in the pit and you get them back by stepping on a pit square again; your deathpile clock is 60 minutes of logged-in time, so go straight back. Decline Kamen's beer, which costs you Agility and hit points each time, and keep four free slots for the witch's chest and one for Klank's gauntlets. The blessed spiders in the ogre cave stay aggressive until your combat level reaches 79, and the three paladins, the three demons and Kalrag can all be safespotted. You can drop the spade, the plank and the spare rope along the way and the tinderbox and bucket after the tomb is burned, which keeps the fights near the end from crowding you. Wear Klank's gauntlets when you search the cage for Iban's dove, which attaches to the doll by itself and takes no slot, and wear a Disciple of Iban's robes (taken from one in the maze) to pass the temple doors. Iban's spikes hit for about 8 each and no prayer blocks them, so keep food for the end.

        UIM riskUse the Iban doll on the well by right-clicking it, never Investigate, and don't drop anything you want back on the way, because the wiki warns you can lose it.
        1. Once Iban is destroyed, Koftik leads you back out of the cavern to the pass entrance; from there go and tell King Lathas in East Ardougne to finish. The wiki notes that all quest items are removed when the quest completes, so the doll and the other pieces the pass hands you won't need slots afterwards; the cat for the witch's house is found inside the pass, so don't bring your own.
        2. Then walk to the Dark Mage in the tower east of the pass entrance in West Ardougne and pay him 200,000 gp to upgrade the staff to Iban's staff (u). That fully charges it to 2,500 casts and completes an Ardougne medium diary task. Later recharges are free at the well inside the pass, or 250,000 gp from him. Treat the 2,500 as a budget, because the free recharge means a trip through the pass.
        Rewards5 quest points, 3,000 Agility XP, 3,000 Attack XP, Iban's staff with the Iban Blast spell, and Klank's gauntlets.

        You have no bank to take a ring of visibility from, so keep the one from Rasolo on you; the ghostspeak amulet joins it in the Lumbridge block below. Later blocks name the steps that need them, and the wiki page for each item says how to replace a lost one.

        Done whenUnderground Pass is complete and Iban's staff (u) holds 2,500 casts.

        Coins: about 520,000 gp · Slots: about 18/28 arriving · Requirements: 25 Ranged and Biohazard for Underground Pass; 50 Magic and 50 Attack to wield the staff

        Start Between a Rock... and buy the gold leaf

        1. Go to Rellekka (a house tablet if you have one left from Marlo's crate, otherwise the Fremennik route you used in 1.3), leave the house through the portal and walk to the Keldagrim cave entrance (the crack between the two dwarf statues).
        2. Take the ferryman across the River Kelda and talk to Dondakan on the rock to start Between a Rock... and continue until it sends you to Rolad. Bring a few coins for the ferry (2 gp a crossing).
        3. In Keldagrim, buy one gold leaf from the Stonemason in the south-west of the city for 130,000 gp. Buy silk at the clothes stall only when the quest that uses it is next.
        Done whenBetween a Rock... has sent you to Rolad and the gold leaf is in your inventory.

        Build the lectern and make house tablets

        RequirementsThe mahogany eagle lectern needs 67 Construction, boostable, two mahogany planks and the gold leaf.

        From a base of 62, a cup of tea made in a porcelain cup at your kitchen shelves (+2) and a crystal saw (+3) reach 67. Tea items vanish when you leave the house, so brew the tea right before you build. Chapter 1 dropped its crystal saw after The Eyes of Glouphrie, so collect a new one first: take the spirit tree from the Grand Exchange to the Tree Gnome Stronghold, ask Brimstail in his cave in the south-west of the Stronghold for a crystal saw seed, and use the seed on the singing bowl in the same cave. The wiki says that costs nothing, gives 28 charges and spends a charge only when you build something that needs the boost. The saw is destroyed on death, so the Recruitment Drive death in the next block takes it; Brimstail gives another seed whenever a later build needs one.

        1. Make the two mahogany planks from two mahogany logs at any sawmill (1,500 gp each). The wiki says the operator won't take noted logs, so unnote the two first at the Woodcutting Guild's bank chest, the way Chapter 1 unnoted its planks there. The chest unnotes as many as fit, so keep only two slots free, or pay Phials in Rimmington 5 gp a log for exactly two.
        2. Build a study, then the lectern.

        Teleport to house tablets need 40 Magic, one law, one air and one earth rune and one soft clay each. Mine clay in the south-west Varrock mine and wet each piece with a bucket of water. One bucket wets one clay and you get the empty bucket back, so back at the house you refill it at the kitchen sink about 24 times a trip (the Humidify spell that waters a whole inventory needs 68 Magic). A bracelet of clay would skip the water, but each one holds only 28 soft clay and is a sapphire bracelet enchanted with Lvl-1 Enchant, so for a hundred tablets the bucket is simpler.

        1. Take about 24 clay a trip, with your pickaxe, the bucket and the rune pouch taking the other slots.
        2. Cast Teleport to House (or break a tablet once you have one), make all 24 at the lectern, and repeat until you have about 100.
        3. Keep one soft clay back for the rod mould in In Aid of the Myreque.

        They stack in one slot and are your teleport for herb and tree runs from here on.

        Done whenthe lectern stands in the study, you hold about 100 house tablets, and one soft clay is set aside.

        Coins: about 370,000 gp · Slots: about 14/28 with the tablet stack · Requirements: 67 Construction (boostable) for the lectern, 40 Magic for the tablets

        Wiki referencesUnderground PassIban's staff (u)Between a Rock...Gold leafMahogany eagle lecternTeleport to house (tablet)

        Rolad and Falador: Recruitment Drive and Wanted!

        This block reads the dwarven lore for Between a Rock..., finishes Recruitment Drive, starts Heroes' Quest and Wanted!, and makes Falador your respawn point. It has the section's first planned death, for Recruitment Drive. Troll Romance used to open this block; it now has its own block after Animal Magnetism's magnet, where its sled materials are picked up on the way. One step moves later for a UIM: the ice gloves. The wiki gives the way into the Ice Queen's lair as the rockslide on White Wolf Mountain, which needs 50 Mining, and you have about 43. They come after the sandstone block, where Mining passes 50.

        Gate inBlack Knights' Fortress (done two blocks ago) and Druidic Ritual; 32 quest points for Wanted!, 55 for Heroes' Quest; 20 noted mahogany logs left after the lectern; 20 Defence and 10 Prayer for the initiate armour.
        Carry inthe house tablets, a pickaxe for the lore pages, the 20 noted mahogany logs and at least 30,000 gp for the Varrock diary planks.
        ExitRecruitment Drive done; respawn set to Falador; 20 mahogany planks made for the Varrock diary; full initiate worn; Heroes' Quest and Wanted! started, with the commorb; about 325,000 gp; about 18/28.
        Carries forwardthe dwarven lore book (Between a Rock...), the ammo mould (the golden cannonball), full initiate (the Altar of Guthix), the commorb (Wanted! and The Slug Menace).

        Read the dwarven lore

        1. Teleport to Falador and walk out to the dwarven camp at the Ice Mountain mine entrance, in the foothills north of the road between Falador and Barbarian Village. Talk to Rolad in the eastern house and continue Between a Rock... until Rolad asks for three book pages from the Dwarven Mine behind the camp. Click through all of his dialogue before you leave, because the pages do not appear if you cut it short.
        2. Take one from the scorpions in the south-east of the mine (level 14; it can take several kills, and the wiki suggests changing worlds if none drops it), search a mine cart along the tracks for the second and get the third by mining clay, copper, tin or iron. The pages can come in any order. The south end of the mine also holds level 32 King Scorpions, which the wiki says trouble lower combat levels, so keep your food handy and walk away from them rather than fight.
        3. Hand them in for the dwarven lore and, when Rolad asks for the book back, choose the option marked as a lie (it follows "Of course") so you keep it for the later steps of the quest.
        4. Buy an ammo mould from Nulodion in the western house, next to Rolad's; the golden cannonball you make at the Al Kharid furnace needs it. It stays in your bag until that visit in the Varrock block, and afterwards you keep it for the cannonball diary task in Port Phasmatys.
        Done whenRolad has the pages, you kept the lore book and the ammo mould is in your inventory.

        Recruitment Drive with the Falador Castle deathpile

        Recruitment Drive needs an empty inventory and nothing worn, and the wiki's UIM note says exactly that. With no bank, the only way to empty a bag full of quest gear is to leave it on the ground, so in the Falador Castle courtyard take off everything and die to a White Knight, as in Chapter 1. Everything lands in a deathpile there, which lasts 60 minutes of logged-in time, and that clock keeps running while you do the quest, so go straight through it. You respawn in Lumbridge with nothing; walk back to Falador, climb the stairs on the west side of White Knights' Castle to the second floor and talk to Sir Amik again, then find Sir Tiffy Cashien on his bench at the entrance to Falador Park and do Recruitment Drive in full. Sir Tiffy sends you through five test rooms in a row, picked at random from seven, and failing any one of them returns you to Falador to start the run again, which costs minutes on the pile's clock. Sir Leye can't be hurt by blades, and finishing him with a bladed weapon fails his room, so make the last blow bare-handed or with the steel warhammer that lies in his room. Two rooms punish impatience: with Sir Tinley you must stand still for eight seconds without clicking, and with Lady Table you get only a few seconds to look at the statues, so study them the moment you walk in.

        Rewards1,000 Prayer, Herblore and Agility XP, 3,000 gp and the Gaze of Saradomin.
        1. Still empty-handed, ask Sir Tiffy to set your respawn point to Falador; the Gaze of Saradomin is what allows it, and the wiki says you can change it later by talking to him again, which you have no reason to do. Walk back to your pile in the courtyard, but take only part of it first: this is the planned death that gives you the free slots for the Varrock diary's medium task, make 20 mahogany planks in one go. The sawmill operator won't take noted logs and the task needs 20 mahogany logs in the inventory at once, plus 30,000 gp (1,500 gp a plank).
        2. Pick up the 20 noted mahogany logs, at least 30,000 gp and a Varrock and a Falador teleport (tablets or runes), unnote the logs at the Falador bank booth, teleport to Varrock and have the sawmill operator at the Lumber Yard east of the city turn all 20 into planks in one go. Leave everything else on the pile: unnoted, the logs fill 20 slots by themselves, and the rest of your gear stays on the ground, on its 60-minute clock, until you come back for it.
        3. Put eight of the planks in your plank sack (one for Cold War, five for Making Friends with My Arm and two for the Fire of Nourishment) and drop the other twelve: the sack holds 28, and the two Myreque quests need 17 of that space. Dropping the twelve is deliberate, since carried they would cost twelve slots for the rest of the block. Teleport back to Falador, then pick up the rest of the pile. The whole errand takes a few minutes, well inside the hour a pile lasts. From now on every death returns you to Falador, a minute from that courtyard.

        Buy full initiate armour from Sir Tiffy: the sallet is free as a reward of the quest, and the cuisse (8,000 gp) and the hauberk (10,000 gp) cost 18,000 gp together. You need it for the Altar of Guthix step at the stone circle in Taverley, so wear it until then; worn pieces take no inventory slot.

        Done whenRecruitment Drive is complete, your respawn is Falador, the 20 mahogany planks are made, the pile is collected and full initiate is worn.

        Start Heroes' Quest and Wanted!

        1. Walk to Burthorpe and start Heroes' Quest with Achietties, who waits outside the Heroes' Guild between Taverley and Burthorpe (55 quest points). She asks for three things, all handed over together: a fire feather from the Entrana firebird, a cooked lava eel and a Master Thieves' armband. The feather is too hot to pick up without ice gloves, which is why the Ice Queen is left for later and the feather waits for the ice gloves block. The lava eel is what the oily fishing rod in the next block is for.
        2. Back in Falador, start Wanted! with Sir Tiffy. The celestial ring waits for the ice gloves block: Dusuri sells it in the Mining Guild, which needs 60 Mining, and you are only about 43 here. Sir Tiffy sends you up to Sir Amik on the second floor of the castle's west side. Refuse Sir Amik's offer to make you a squire: accepting it traps you in a loop of Asgarnian ale requests that only Sir Tiffy can undo. Go back to Sir Tiffy, who can need several conversations before he mentions a crisis, so keep talking to him, then return to Sir Amik, join the White Knights and accept the mission to hunt Solus Dellagar.
        3. Get the commorb from him: 10,000 gp, or a law rune, a molten glass and an enchanted gem. Continue Wanted! until it sends you to Taverley Dungeon. You need the commorb for the whole quest, because Savant speaks to you through it and is the one who points you to the Black Knights' hideout in Taverley Dungeon and to the Mage of Zamorak in Varrock. If you lose it, the wiki says Sir Tiffy lends you his.
        Done whenHeroes' Quest is started and Wanted! has sent you towards Taverley Dungeon with the commorb.

        Coins: about 325,000 gp · Slots: about 18/28 after the pile · Requirements: Black Knights' Fortress and Druidic Ritual for Recruitment Drive; 32 quest points and the listed quests for Wanted!; 55 quest points for Heroes' Quest

        Wiki referencesRecruitment DriveWanted!Heroes' QuestIce Queen

        Shilo Village, the Shilo gems and the first leg of One Small Favour

        This block takes you from Karamja back to Draynor and Lumbridge in one sweep. It finishes Shilo Village, mines every gem the next part of the chapter needs, carries One Small Favour as far as Seth Groats and finishes Death to the Dorgeshuun. You can't get the Heroes' Quest fire feather from Entrana here, because the feather has to be picked up wearing ice gloves and the Ice Queen needs 50 Mining. Entrana moves to the block after the sandstone mining.

        Gate inJungle Potion, 20 Crafting and 32 Agility for Shilo Village; 36 Agility, 25 Crafting, 18 Herblore and 30 Smithing for One Small Favour; The Lost Tribe, 23 Agility and 23 Thieving for Death to the Dorgeshuun.
        Carry inslots rather than items for Shilo Village (Jiminua's store sells most of them), the bronze wire, three bones and food: about 22/28 in the H.A.M. hideout.
        ExitShilo Village and Death to the Dorgeshuun done; One Small Favour stopped at Seth Groats; the gems cut; two H.A.M. robe sets (one worn); the oily fishing rod; about 325,000 gp; about 15/28.
        Carries forwardthe cut jade and red topaz (Al Kharid furnace), the cut sapphire (the lantern), one polished button (Animal Magnetism), the oily fishing rod and bait (the lava eel), three clean harralanders and one grimy, two gold bars (the silver is mined at Al Kharid two blocks later), the ghostspeak amulet.

        Shilo Village with a light inventory

        The wiki lists a spade, a lit candle or torch, a rope, a chisel, bronze wire and three regular bones for Shilo Village, plus a tinderbox to relight the candle. Everything except the bones and the wire is sold at Jiminua's Jungle Store, north of Tai Bwo Wannai, for under 100 coins, so arrive with the slots, not the items. Smelt one bronze bar from a copper and a tin ore (the Rimmington mine on the way to Port Sarim has both; Chapter 1 carries no bars) and smith it into bronze wire (4 Smithing) before you leave the mainland. If you reach Shilo without the wire, Jiminua also sells a bronze bar and a hammer for 13 coins together, and the wiki says there is an anvil nearby, so a forgotten wire costs a short detour and not the quest. The quest begins with Mosol Rei outside the village, but it only properly starts when you give the Wampum belt to Trufitus in the north-eastern house of Tai Bwo Wannai and pick the right dialogue options; the wiki asks you to keep one slot free then. The candle or torch is used up when you light the fissure above the Temple of Ah Za Rhoon, and the wiki says a bullseye lantern does not work for it, so carry a candle or torch, not a lantern.

        The fight is Nazastarool in three forms, at levels 91, 68 and 93, after a run of undead ones (levels 61–73). The wiki says each form can be safespotted, and recommends Crumble Undead with Protect from Melee. Its safespot is to lure him to the northern part of the dolmen and run around it until he gets stuck, and each form despawns after five minutes. Put on the Beads of the Dead before you enter the tomb (the bronze wire is what turns the bone beads into them): without them you can't continue and you take constant damage. Leave the stack of coins on the floor, because picking it up spawns more undead. When you have the Rashiliyia corpse, leave with the bone key on the doors, since walking out the way you came in makes Rashiliyia summon three undead ones. The tomb doors remember how many of the three bones you have used, so a death midway costs the walk back and not the bones. With your respawn in Falador, a death puts your pile in the tomb, a boat trip from Falador, with the 60 minutes running while you travel. Stop at the Shrimp and Parrot in Brimhaven on the way south and buy cooked karambwans from Alfonse the waiter. He sells them as cooked food, but he stocks only 3 at 325 gp each, so bring other food and buy only what this fight and the next few need: cooked food vanishes from a deathpile, and a death here would leave you respawning in Falador.

        When the quest is done, sell every leftover quest item to Yanni Salika in Shilo Village. The wiki gives about 2,000 coins for the lot (100 coins each for the scrolls, plaque, notes and bone key, 500 for the locating crystal and 1,000 for the beads), and it gives you those slots back. Until you reach Yanni, keep the quest items instead of dropping them for room: the wiki says a dropped sword pommel or set of beads turns to dust and you have to go back for new ones, a dropped locating crystal hurts for 10 damage, and lost Bervirius notes have to be written again with charcoal and papyrus from Jiminua's store. You also get access to the village and its gem rocks, the Brimhaven–Shilo cart (a fare of 10 to 200 coins, set by how many coins you carry) and charters on the Lady of the Waves to Port Khazard or Port Sarim.

        Rewards2 quest points and 3,875 Crafting XP.
        Done whenShilo Village is complete and its leftover quest items are sold.

        Mine and cut the gems you need

        Mine the gem rocks in Shilo Village until you have what the next blocks use, cutting as you go and dropping or selling whatever you don't need:

        • one cut jade, for the necklace of passage you make at the Al Kharid furnace two blocks later;
        • three cut red topaz: two become Efaritay's aid rings at the Al Kharid furnace, and the third is a spare, so keep it and sell it if nothing has used it by Sins of the Father;
        • one cut sapphire, for the sapphire lantern in Tears of Guthix (next block);
        • not the machete's gems: the opal machete is bought in 2.2's Tai Bwo Wannai block, a cart ride from these rocks, so you mine its three opals and trade gems then instead of carrying them for most of 2.1.

        The gem rocks are in the north-west of Shilo Village and need 40 Mining. Each success gives one uncut gem, and the wiki's odds are that of every 128 gems 60 are opals, 30 jade, 15 red topaz and 9 sapphires, so the red topaz and the sapphire are the slow ones. That is why you mine until the count is met and not for a set time. Jade, opal and red topaz can be crushed while cutting, so cut until you have the count, not a fixed number of gems. You don't need spares for One Small Favour: its landing lights hand you uncut gems on the spot, and Gnormadium sells up to two replacements of each type for 500 coins each if you keep the crushed pieces. That costs coins instead of carrying slots for hours.

        Done whenyou hold the jade, three red topaz and the sapphire.

        One Small Favour from Shilo to Port Sarim

        1. Talk to Yanni Salika in the antiques shop north of the river to start One Small Favour. He wants red mahogany logs, so go through the village's south-eastern gate, climbing over the broken cart, and find the Jungle Forester just south-west of the gate, who gives you a blunt axe to have sharpened.
        2. Get to Cairn Isle on the south-west coast of Karamja, where Captain Shanks's Lady of the Waves docks south of the island (the wiki lists no Shilo departure); buy a ship ticket from him on the spot (the price is randomised between 20 and 50 gp each time you ask) and sail to Port Khazard, which is a medium Karamja diary task (the boat is one-way), then charter from Port Khazard to Port Sarim.
        3. In Port Sarim, Brian at the axe shop takes the blunt axe and wants Aggie as a character witness for his friend. Agree, and check that he has taken the axe before you carry on, as the wiki stresses.

        While in Port Sarim, ask Gerrant at the fishing shop for a bottle of blamish snail slime for Heroes' Quest; he gives it free. Ask now: if you lose the slime later and Gerrant won't give a second bottle, the wiki's fallback is to kill a Myre blamish snail and grind its meat with a pestle and mortar into an empty sample bottle (Fidelio's general store in Canifis sells them). Buy a fishing rod from him as well, and a stack of fishing bait for the lava eel in Taverley Dungeon. The bait stacks, so it costs one slot.

        You need two gold bars over the next blocks, for Dondakan in Between a Rock... and the golden cannonball, and six silver bars: three for the necklace of passage and the two topaz rings, one for the silver sickle that In Search of the Myreque needs blessed (Chapter 1 dropped its sickle after Nature Spirit), one for the Ectofuntus silver dust and one for the silvthrill rod in 2.2: jade and red topaz jewellery is made with silver, not gold. The holy symbol for Animal Magnetism is cast later, in the Family Crest block, from ore mined next to the Al Kharid furnace. Chapter 1 carries no gold bars, so mine two gold ore at Rimmington mine (40 Mining) on the walk from Port Sarim to Draynor and smelt them at the Lumbridge furnace. Rimmington has no silver, so mine the six silver ore in the Al Kharid mine, next to the furnace where the rings, the necklace and the sickle are made.

        Done whenBrian has the blunt axe, and you hold the snail slime, a fishing rod, the bait and the two gold bars.

        Draynor, Lumbridge and the H.A.M. hideout

        In Draynor, buy the Chronicle back from Diango in the market for 300 gp: the Recruitment Drive death destroyed it, and its 100 charges come back with it. Talk to Aggie for One Small Favour: she will witness for Brian's friend only if you look into the H.A.M. hideout for her missing apprentice, Jimmy the Chisel, which is the trip you make next. Make a harralander potion (unf), add the blamish snail slime to make blamish oil (25 Herblore) and use the oil on the fishing rod to get the oily fishing rod. You need four harralanders over the chapter: this one, one for the combat potion in Nardah, one for the Guthix rest in One Small Favour and one for the Guthix balance in In Aid of the Myreque. Take them from your herb runs. Carry three clean, and leave the Nardah one grimy, because Zahur cleaning a herb is a diary task there.

        Go to the Lumbridge Castle cellar and continue Death to the Dorgeshuun (Chapter 1 started it with Mistag) before you go near the H.A.M. members. The quest needs a light source the whole way, so buy a spare candle at Jiminua's with the Shilo items and keep the tinderbox; the wiki recommends a lantern, because an open candle or torch can set off the swamp caves' gas. Zanik appears by the ladder in the cellar once Mistag has finished with you and then follows you like a pet; the wiki says to dismiss any pet of your own first, and if you teleport she goes back to the cellar and you have to talk to her again to carry on. Robes drop much more often while the quest is running, and you need two full seven-piece sets: one for you and one for Zanik. Then pick the trapdoor lock into the H.A.M. hideout and talk to Johanhus Ulsbrecht, in the south-eastern corner, for One Small Favour: he is holding Jimmy hostage and wants a month's supply of chickens. Pickpocket H.A.M. members until both sets are complete, and keep one polished button for Animal Magnetism later in the chapter. Wear one set and carry the other; all seven pieces are wearable, so the carried set is seven slots and the worn set is none. Drop the rest of the loot as it comes.

        Chapter 1 didn't carry the ghostspeak amulet, and Ghosts Ahoy, Animal Magnetism and Curse of the Empty Lord need one. The wiki says Father Urhney, in his house in the Lumbridge Swamp, gives a new one to anyone who has lost theirs, so collect it on this visit; wear it in place of the amulet of accuracy whenever you'd rather not spend the slot.

        Talk to Fred the Farmer in the house north-east of the hideout; he has too few chickens and sends you to Seth Groats, who is by the chicken coop on the east side of the river. Seth needs chicken cages from Horvik in Varrock and three steel bars delivered to Horvik. You carry that on in the Varrock block, so stop One Small Favour there.

        Keep the cut jade for the necklace of passage: you make it at the Al Kharid furnace with the topaz rings, from a silver bar, the jade and a necklace mould (25 Crafting), and enchant it with Lvl-2 Enchant. Spirits of the Elid uses it later for Eagles' Eyrie.

        Back in the cellar, complete Death to the Dorgeshuun. Light your mining helmet in the cellar for the diary along the way. The hideout part ends in a cell: Sigmund has taken Zanik outside and she is dead, so you pick the lock, carry her body to Juna in the Chasm of Tears and revive her by collecting 20 tears with her bowl. That gives no XP and does not count as your weekly Tears of Guthix game. Because Tears of Guthix is not done yet, you reach her through the Lumbridge Swamp caves and over the stepping stone, and the wiki says failing the crossing puts your light out; if you die while holding Zanik she respawns at the hideout. Wear the full H.A.M. outfit for the next stage, search an empty southern crate at the chicken farm near the water mill (not one with machine parts) because Zanik is hiding in it, and carry it with empty hands to the trapdoor without wandering far, or she leaves it. The Sigmund fight (level 50, with three level 22 guards) is in an instance. At low levels the wiki has you kill the guards first; you only need to beat Sigmund, who escapes at 1 Hitpoint, and then smash the drilling machine in the same room and leave by the southern tunnel, because the cutscene only plays if you do. The wiki also warns that a pet can be lost when you leave just before the fight. Your cat is in the menagerie, so that doesn't affect you.

        UIM riskIf you die there, your items go to a deathpile outside the instance and you respawn in Falador; anything left on the ground inside the instance is lost, so collect the pile at once.
        Rewards2,000 Thieving and 2,000 Ranged XP and access to Dorgesh-Kaan.
        Done whenthe oily fishing rod is made, both H.A.M. sets are complete, the ghostspeak amulet is collected, Seth Groats is reached and Death to the Dorgeshuun is done.

        Coins: about 325,000 gp · Slots: about 22/28 in the H.A.M. hideout, 15/28 after the quest · Requirements: Jungle Potion, 20 Crafting and 32 Agility for Shilo Village; Shilo Village, Rune Mysteries, Druidic Ritual, 36 Agility, 25 Crafting, 18 Herblore and 30 Smithing for One Small Favour; 25 Herblore for the blamish oil; The Lost Tribe, 23 Agility and 23 Thieving for Death to the Dorgeshuun

        Wiki referencesShilo VillageAlfonse the waiterOne Small FavourHeroes' QuestDeath to the DorgeshuunPolished buttons

        Dorgesh-Kaan, Another Slice of H.A.M. and Tears of Guthix

        This block stays underground under Lumbridge and finishes three quests in the Lumbridge Swamp Caves and Dorgesh-Kaan. It ends with the train to Keldagrim and Dondakan.

        Gate inDeath to the Dorgeshuun, The Giant Dwarf and The Dig Site; 15 Attack and 25 Prayer (not boostable); 43 quest points, 49 Firemaking, 20 Crafting and 20 Mining for Tears of Guthix; a lockpick (bought at the start of this block); the cut sapphire.
        Carry inRanged or Magic for the two H.A.M. fighters, eight free slots, a pickaxe, a chisel and a tinderbox: about 16/28.
        ExitAnother Slice of H.A.M. and Tears of Guthix done (minigame not played yet); the lit sapphire lantern; Between a Rock... advanced at Dondakan; about 320,000 gp.
        Carries forwardthe sapphire lantern and the lantern lens, the Dorgesh-Kaan sphere (Wanted!), the ancient mace.

        Prepare the sapphire lantern in Dorgesh-Kaan

        Tears of Guthix needs a lit sapphire lantern, which is made from a bullseye lantern. Miltog in the Dorgesh-Kaan marketplace sells empty bullseye lanterns, one in stock at a time, and his shop has a lamp oil still. Buy one, fill it with lamp oil at the still (it distils swamp tar: Chapter 1 carried none, so pick one from the spawns in the Lumbridge Swamp on your way to the cave entrance) and use your cut sapphire on it. If the lantern has a lens, using the sapphire on it gives you that lens back: keep the lantern lens, because it is what swaps the sapphire back out of the lantern if a later step wants a plain bullseye lantern. If you don't get one, blow a lantern lens from a molten glass (49 Crafting) before 2.2. Light the lantern with a tinderbox (49 Firemaking). Do the swap and the lighting before you go into the caves: the wiki says a bullseye lantern can't be put out once you are in the dungeon or the chasm. Swamp gas in the north-west and south-middle parts of the caves sets off open lights such as candles and torches, so do not rely on a spare one of those. The sapphire lantern is also your light source for the rest of this block.

        Loot a Dorgesh-Kaan average chest and a rich chest here with a lockpick. Chapter 1 carried none, so before you go underground take the games necklace to Burthorpe and buy one at Martin Thwait's Lost and Found in the Rogues' Den (20 gp; the den needs 50 Thieving and 50 Agility, and the shop is outside the maze), then cast Lumbridge Home Teleport back. The average chest (52 Thieving) drops a bullseye lantern at 1/7, so try a few before paying Miltog. There are chests on three floors of the city (five on the ground floor, six on each floor above), each pays 200 Thieving XP and refills about every 90 seconds, so you can work two in turn instead of waiting. Coins, oil lanterns, mining helmets and cave goblin wire make up the rest of what they pay, so drop what you don't use. Then loot one rich chest (78 Thieving, 650 Thieving XP, refilling about every 300 seconds), which is a Lumbridge & Draynor elite diary task. Its table is a spread of uncut gems and odds and ends, so its gems are worth keeping only if they fill a gap in the Shilo list.

        From Oldak, buy one Dorgesh-Kaan sphere: one molten glass and two law runes. Wanted! uses it later. Kill cave goblins for Rag and Bone Man II while you are in the city.

        Done whenthe sapphire lantern is lit, the rich chest is looted and the sphere is bought.

        Another Slice of H.A.M.

        RequirementsAnother Slice of H.A.M: 15 Attack and 25 Prayer, neither boostable, and Ranged or Magic against the two level 30 fighters, which melee can't hurt (Sigmund can be fought in any style).

        Another Slice of H.A.M. starts with Ur-tag in Dorgesh-Kaan. If you have no ranged weapon for the tower, a bronze crossbow and 50 bronze bolts spawn upstairs in it; leaving and re-entering the instance respawns the bolts, but it also resets the enemies. The H.A.M. mage and archer (level 30) in the goblin village tower hit at the same time and never miss. Hiding behind the crates stops their damage, and you can attack one and step back behind the crates straight away. Dismiss any follower before you take Zanik, since teleporting leaves her behind and entering the Dorgesh-Kaan Mine makes her abandon you. For the guard section you can't use the Dorgesh-Kaan entrance: go in through the Lumbridge Swamp caves with the two goblin sergeants and use them as decoys. Against Sigmund (level 64), use the ancient mace's special attack to stop his protection prayers for the rest of the fight. The wiki notes it can fail if used too early. The wiki recommends eight free slots; the trowel and specimen brush are handed to you during the quest.

        UIM riskThe H.A.M.-bush, guard-evasion and Sigmund sections are instances: a death there leaves your items in a deathpile outside the instance, and anything left on the ground inside is lost, so go back for it at once.
        Rewards3,000 Mining and 3,000 Prayer XP, the ancient mace, Goblin Village teleport spheres and free use of the Dorgesh-Kaan–Keldagrim train.
        Done whenAnother Slice of H.A.M. is complete and the Dorgesh-Kaan–Keldagrim train is open.

        Tears of Guthix in the same caves

        Do Tears of Guthix while you are in the Lumbridge Swamp Caves. Talk to Juna, deep in the Chasm of Tears, then use the lit sapphire lantern on a light creature to cross the chasm. The wiki calls the route from the Lumbridge Castle cellar the faster and safer one, with no wall beasts, cave crawlers or swamp gas, and it goes south over a stepping stone to the chasm. Tell Juna a story before you try for the bowl, then mine a magic stone and chisel it into a stone bowl (20 Crafting), cross back and give the bowl to Juna. One bowl is enough. You need a pickaxe and a chisel; a rope is needed only if you have never been into the caves. Don't play the minigame yet: the next block's first visit is the one that starts your weekly routine.

        Rewards1,000 Crafting XP and weekly access to the minigame.
        Done whenTears of Guthix is complete, with the minigame not yet played.

        Keldagrim and Dondakan
